VI.2.12]). Proceeding similarly to the proof of [20, Prop. IX.1.12]—using (2.10) to be able to approximate the expectation of the processes through the expectation of their truncated alterations—guarantees that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Z_{\bullet \, \wedge \, \tau _a(Z)}$$\end{document}Z∙∧τa(Z) is a martingale, and hence Z is a local martingale. Thus, by [20, Prop. I.4.50d], Z=YZ=Y follows. □ □

Proof of Proposition 2.15

To prove Proposition 2.15, we first need an auxiliary result. It will help us to construct convenient parametric representations of the simple integrals. For the definition of parametric representations, as they are employed in the definition of the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M_1$$\end{document}M1 metric, see Appendix A.

Proposition 6.5

(Parametric representation of simple integral) Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x \in \textbf{D}_{\mathbb {R}}[0,T]$$\end{document}x∈DR[0,T] be such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x(\bullet \,-)$$\end{document}x(∙-) is a simple left-continuous path as in (2.12). Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$y \in \textbf{D}_{\mathbb {R}}[0,T]$$\end{document}y∈DR[0,T]. Given any parametric representation \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(u,r) \in \Pi (x,y)$$\end{document}(u,r)∈Π(x,y) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u=(u^{(1)}, u^{(2)})$$\end{document}u=(u(1),u(2)), we define6.8\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \tilde{u}(z)&{:}= \sum _{i=1}^k u^{(1)}(\underline{z}_{i+1}) \Big [u^{(2)}(z \wedge {\bar{z}}_{i+1}) - u^{(2)}(z \wedge {\bar{z}}_{i}))\Big ] \end{aligned}$$\end{document}u~(z):=∑i=1ku(1)(z_i+1)[u(2)(z∧z¯i+1)-u(2)(z∧z¯i))]where and . Then, it holds that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\big ((u^{(1)},u^{(2)}, \tilde{u}),\, r\big ) \, \in \, \Pi \big (x, \,y, \, \int _0^\bullet \, x(s \, -) \, \textrm{d}y(s)\big )$$\end{document}((u(1),u(2),u~),r)∈Π(x,y,∫0∙x(s-)dy(s)).

Proof

Let f denote the simple integral of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x(\bullet \,-)$$\end{document}x(∙-) with respect to y. Note that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {Disc}(f) \subseteq \text {Disc}(y)$$\end{document}Disc(f)⊆Disc(y). We begin the proof by showing that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$((u^{(1)}, u^{(2)}, \tilde{u}),r)$$\end{document}((u(1),u(2),u~),r) maps onto the completed graph \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Gamma _{(x,\, y,\,f)}$$\end{document}Γ(x,y,f) (see Appendix A for a definition of the completed graph). Let first \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s \in \text {Disc}(x)^\complement \cap \text {Disc}(y)^\complement $$\end{document}s∈Disc(x)∁∩Disc(y)∁, then there exists \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$z \in [0,\,1]$$\end{document}z∈[0,1] such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$((u^{(1)},u^{(2)}),r) (z) = ((x(s),y(s)),s)$$\end{document}((u(1),u(2)),r)(z)=((x(s),y(s)),s). In particular, there exists \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$j \in \{1,...,k\}$$\end{document}j∈{1,...,k} such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s \in (t_j, t_{j+1})$$\end{document}s∈(tj,tj+1) and by definition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$z \in (\bar{z}_j,\, \underline{z}_{j+1})$$\end{document}z∈(z¯j,z_j+1). Then,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \tilde{u}(z)&=\sum _{i=1}^j u^{(1)}(\underline{z}_{i+1}) \left[ u^{(2)}(z \wedge {\bar{z}}_{i+1}) - u^{(2)}(z \wedge {\bar{z}}_{i})\right] \\ &= \sum _{i=1}^j x(t_i) \left[ y(s \wedge t_{i+1}) - y(s \wedge t_{i})\right] = f(s). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}u~(z)=∑i=1ju(1)(z_i+1)u(2)(z∧z¯i+1)-u(2)(z∧z¯i)=∑i=1jx(ti)y(s∧ti+1)-y(s∧ti)=f(s).Let now \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s \in \text {Disc}(x) \cap \text {Disc}(y)$$\end{document}s∈Disc(x)∩Disc(y), implying \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s = t_{j+1}$$\end{document}s=tj+1 for some \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$j \in \{1,\ldots ,k\}$$\end{document}j∈{1,…,k}. In addition, let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha \in [0,1]$$\end{document}α∈[0,1] and choose z ∈ [0,1]z∈[0,1] such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r(z) = t_{j+1}$$\end{document}r(z)=tj+1 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(u^{(1)},u^{(2)})(z) = \alpha (x,y)(t_{j+1}-) + (1 - \alpha ) (x,y)(t_{j+1})$$\end{document}(u(1),u(2))(z)=α(x,y)(tj+1-)+(1-α)(x,y)(tj+1). By definition, we have that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$z \in [\underline{z}_{j+1},\, {\bar{z}}_{j+1}]$$\end{document}z∈[z_j+1,z¯j+1]. Therefore, we deduce\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \tilde{u}(z) \;&= \; \sum _{i=1}^{j-1} u^{(1)}(\underline{z}_{i+1}) \left[ u^{(2)}({\bar{z}}_{i+1}) - u^{(2)}( {\bar{z}}_{i})\right] \; + \; u^{(1)}(\underline{z}_{j+1}) \left[ u^{(2)}(z) - u^{(2)}( {\bar{z}}_{j})\right] \\&= \; \sum _{i=1}^{j-1} x(t_i) \left[ y(t_{i+1}) - y(t_{i})\right] \; + \; x(t_j) \left[ \alpha y(t_{j+1}-) + (1 - \alpha ) y(t_{j+1}) - y(t_{j})\right] \\&= \alpha \Big (\sum _{i=1}^{j-1} x(t_i) \left[ y(t_{i+1}) - y(t_{i})\right] + x(t_j) \left[ y(t_{j+1}-) - y(t_{j})\right] \Big ) \\&\quad \, + (1-\alpha ) \Big (\sum _{i=1}^{j-1} x(t_i) \left[ y(t_{i+1}) - y(t_{i})\right] + x(t_j) \left[ y(t_{j+1}) - y(t_{j})\right] \Big ) \\&= \alpha f(t_{j+1}-) + (1-\alpha )f(t_{j+1})\, . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}u~(z)=∑i=1j-1u(1)(z_i+1)u(2)(z¯i+1)-u(2)(z¯i)+u(1)(z_j+1)u(2)(z)-u(2)(z¯j)=∑i=1j-1x(ti)y(ti+1)-y(ti)+x(tj)αy(tj+1-)+(1-α)y(tj+1)-y(tj)=α(∑i=1j-1x(ti)y(ti+1)-y(ti)+x(tj)y(tj+1-)-y(tj))+(1-α)(∑i=1j-1x(ti)y(ti+1)-y(ti)+x(tj)y(tj+1)-y(tj))=αf(tj+1-)+(1-α)f(tj+1).The cases \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s \in \text {Disc}(x)^\complement \cap \text {Disc}(y)$$\end{document}s∈Disc(x)∁∩Disc(y) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s \in \text {Disc}(x) \cap \text {Disc}(y)^\complement $$\end{document}s∈Disc(x)∩Disc(y)∁ follow analogously. Hence, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$((u^{(1)}, u^{(2)},\tilde{u}),r): [0,1] \rightarrow \Gamma _{(x,y,f)}$$\end{document}((u(1),u(2),u~),r):[0,1]→Γ(x,y,f) is surjective. Clearly, the continuity of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$((u^{(1)}, u^{(2)},\tilde{u}),r)$$\end{document}((u(1),u(2),u~),r) can easily be derived from the continuity of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$((u^{(1)}, u^{(2)}), r)$$\end{document}((u(1),u(2)),r). Finally, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$((u^{(1)}, u^{(2)},\tilde{u}),r)$$\end{document}((u(1),u(2),u~),r) being non-decreasing with respect to the ordering on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Gamma _{(x,y,f)}$$\end{document}Γ(x,y,f) follows directly from the fact that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$((u^{(1)},u^{(2)}),r) \in \Pi (x,y)$$\end{document}((u(1),u(2)),r)∈Π(x,y) is non-decreasing, and that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$((u^{(1)},u^{(2)},\tilde{u}),r)$$\end{document}((u(1),u(2),u~),r) is onto. □ □

We can now prove the pathwise convergence result for simple Itô integrals.

Proof of Proposition 2.15

We only consider d=1d=1. The general case follows analogously by first extending Proposition 6.5 in a straightforward way to a multidimensional case and then proceeding similarly to the one-dimensional proof. Let \varepsilon > 0ε>0. Since x and y have no common discontinuities, it is well-known that in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\textbf{D}_{{\mathbb {R}}^2}[0,T], \, d_{M_1})$$\end{document}(DR2[0,T],dM1) (see e.g. [51, Thm. 12.6.1]). Hence, there is a sequence of parametric representations \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(u_n,r_n)=((u^{(1)}_n, u^{(2)}_n), r_n) \in \Pi (x_n,y_n)$$\end{document}(un,rn)=((un(1),un(2)),rn)∈Π(xn,yn) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(u,r)=((u^{(1)}, u^{(2)}), r) \in \Pi (x,y)$$\end{document}(u,r)=((u(1),u(2)),r)∈Π(x,y) such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|u_n-u|^*_1\vee |r_n-r|^*_1 \rightarrow 0$$\end{document}|un-u|1∗∨|rn-r|1∗→0. Thus, there is an \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N \in {\mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}N∈N so that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|u_n-u|_1^* \; \le \; \varepsilon [4\tilde{K}(|u^{(1)}|^*_1 + |u^{(2)}|^*_1 + 1)]^{-1}$$\end{document}|un-u|1∗≤ε[4K~(|u(1)|1∗+|u(2)|1∗+1)]-1 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|u_n^{(1)}|^*_1 \le |u^{(1)}|^*_1 + 1$$\end{document}|un(1)|1∗≤|u(1)|1∗+1 for every n \ge Nn≥N. Employing Proposition 6.5, we know that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$((u^{(1)}, u^{(2)},\tilde{u}),r)$$\end{document}((u(1),u(2),u~),r) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$((u^{(1)}_n, u^{(2)}_n,\tilde{u}_n),r_n)$$\end{document}((un(1),un(2),u~n),rn) are parametrisations of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x,y,\int _0^\bullet x(s-)\textrm{d}y(s))$$\end{document}(x,y,∫0∙x(s-)dy(s)) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x_n,y_n,\int _0^\bullet x_n(s-)\textrm{d}y_n(s))$$\end{document}(xn,yn,∫0∙xn(s-)dyn(s)) respectively, taking the specific form (6.8). We denote \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t^n_1,...,t^n_{k_n}$$\end{document}t1n,...,tnn the discontinuity times of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x_n$$\end{document}xn, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\underline{z}^n_{i}, {\bar{z}}^n_{i}, \tilde{u}_n, u_n^{(1)},u_n^{(2)}$$\end{document}z_in,z¯in,u~n,un(1),un(2) the specific quantities from (6.8) associated to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x_n,y_n)$$\end{document}(xn,yn). Analogously, we omit the index n to denote the respective quantities for (x, y).

We only need to show \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ |\tilde{u}_n-\tilde{u}|^*_1 \rightarrow 0$$\end{document}|u~n-u~|1∗→0 as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n\rightarrow \infty $$\end{document}n→∞ in order to deduce \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x_n, y_n, \int _0^\bullet x_n(s-)\textrm{d}y_n(s))$$\end{document}(xn,yn,∫0∙xn(s-)dyn(s)) \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rightarrow (x, y, \int _0^\bullet x(s-)\textrm{d}y(s))$$\end{document}→(x,y,∫0∙x(s-)dy(s)) in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\textbf{D}_{{\mathbb {R}}^3}[0,T], d_{M_1})$$\end{document}(DR3[0,T],dM1). We will do this by induction over intervals to each of the jumps of x. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$z \in [0,\,{\bar{z}}_2]$$\end{document}z∈[0,z¯2]. We recall that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_1 \equiv u^{(1)}(\underline{z}_2)$$\end{document}u1≡u(1)(z_2) on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$[0,\, \underline{z}_2] $$\end{document}[0,z_2] and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r(\underline{z}_2) = t_2$$\end{document}r(z_2)=t2. Furthermore, by definition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t_1 = 0 = {\bar{z}}_1 = {\bar{z}}^n_1 $$\end{document}t1=0=z¯1=z¯1n and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^{(2)}(\underline{z}_1) = u^{(2)}({\bar{z}}_1)$$\end{document}u(2)(z_1)=u(2)(z¯1) since x and y do not jump at the same time by assumption. Now, we can write \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\tilde{u}(z) - \tilde{u}_{n}(z)|$$\end{document}|u~(z)-u~n(z)| as\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \Big |u^{(1)}(\underline{z}_2)\left[ u^{(2)}(z)-u^{(2)}({\bar{z}}_1)\right] - \sum _{i=1}^{k(2,n)} u_n^{(1)}(\underline{z}^n_{i+1}) \Big [u_n^{(2)}(z \wedge {\bar{z}}^n_{i+1}) - u_n^{(2)}(z \wedge {\bar{z}}^n_{i})\Big ]\Big |$$\end{document}|u(1)(z_2)u(2)(z)-u(2)(z¯1)-∑i=1k(2,n)un(1)(z_i+1n)[un(2)(z∧z¯i+1n)-un(2)(z∧z¯in)]|where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t_1^n, \ldots , t_{k(2,n)}^n$$\end{document}t1n,…,tk(2,n)n are jump times of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x_n$$\end{document}xn with k(2, n) being minimal such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{z}_2 \le {\bar{z}}_{k(2,n)}^n$$\end{document}z¯2≤z¯k(2,n)n. We rewrite the first summand \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{u}(z)$$\end{document}u~(z) using a telescoping sum to obtain that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{u}(z) - \tilde{u}_n(z)$$\end{document}u~(z)-u~n(z) equals\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\sum _{i=1}^{k(2,n)-1} u^{(1)}(\underline{z}_{2}) \Big [u^{(2)}(z \wedge {\bar{z}}^n_{i+1}) - u^{(2)}(z \wedge {\bar{z}}^n_{i})\Big ]\\ &\quad - \sum _{i=1}^{k(2,n)-1} u_n^{(1)}(\underline{z}^n_{i+1})\Big [u_n^{(2)}(z \wedge {\bar{z}}^n_{i+1}) - u_n^{(2)}(z \wedge {\bar{z}}^n_{i})\Big ] \\ &\quad + u^{(1)}(\underline{z}_{2}) \Big [u^{(2)}(z \wedge {\bar{z}}^n_{1}) - u^{(2)}(z \wedge {\bar{z}}_{1})\Big ] . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∑i=1k(2,n)-1u(1)(z_2)[u(2)(z∧z¯i+1n)-u(2)(z∧z¯in)]-∑i=1k(2,n)-1un(1)(z_i+1n)[un(2)(z∧z¯i+1n)-un(2)(z∧z¯in)]+u(1)(z_2)[u(2)(z∧z¯1n)-u(2)(z∧z¯1)].The last term of this sum equals zero as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\bar{z}}_1 = {\bar{z}}^n_1=0$$\end{document}z¯1=z¯1n=0. Hence, using the fundamental inequality \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|ab-a_n b_n| \le |a_n||b_n - b| + |b| |a_n - a|$$\end{document}|ab-anbn|≤|an||bn-b|+|b||an-a| we obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} |\tilde{u}(z) - \tilde{u}_n(z)| \;&\le \; \sum _{i=1}^{k(2,n)-1} |u_n^{(1)}(\underline{z}^n_{i+1})| \cdot \Bigl |u_n^{(2)}(z \wedge {\bar{z}}^n_{i+1}) - u_n^{(2)}(z \wedge {\bar{z}}^n_{i}) - \\&u^{(2)}(z \wedge {\bar{z}}^n_{i+1}) + u^{(2)}(z \wedge {\bar{z}}^n_{i})\Bigr | \\&\qquad + \sum _{i=1}^{k(2,n)-1} \Big |u^{(2)}(z \wedge {\bar{z}}^n_{i+1}) - u^{(2)}(z \wedge {\bar{z}}^n_{i})\Big | \cdot |u_n^{(1)}(\underline{z}^n_{i+1}) - u^{(1)}(\underline{z}_{2})| \\&\le \; Remark 6.6

If we have another sequence of càdlàg functions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$z_n$$\end{document}zn such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(y_n,z_n) \rightarrow (y,z)$$\end{document}(yn,zn)→(y,z) in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\textbf{D}_{{\mathbb {R}}^{2d}}[0,T], d_{M_1})$$\end{document}(DR2d[0,T],dM1), then it is immediate from the proof above that one obtains\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Bigl (x_n,y_n,z_n, \int _0^\bullet \! x_n(s-)\textrm{d}y_n(s)\Bigr ) \longrightarrow \Bigl (x,y,z, \int _0^\bullet \! x(s-) \textrm{d}y(s)\Bigr )$$\end{document}(xn,yn,zn,∫0∙xn(s-)dyn(s))⟶(x,y,z,∫0∙x(s-)dy(s))on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\textbf{D}_{{\mathbb {R}}^{4d}}[0,\,T], d_{M_1})$$\end{document}(DR4d[0,T],dM1) as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n\rightarrow \infty $$\end{document}n→∞.

Proofs of the results from section 3

Here we give the proofs of the results in Section 3, in the same order as they were presented.

Relatively compact local martingales in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M_1$$\end{document}M1

We begin by proving an alternative tightness criterion for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$J_1$$\end{document}J1 akin to Aldous’ criterion [5, Thm. 16.10]. Denote by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$D\subseteq [0,\infty )$$\end{document}D⊆[0,∞), with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$D \ne \emptyset $$\end{document}D≠∅, any set such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {sup}(D)=+\infty $$\end{document}sup(D)=+∞.

Proof

Assume the conditions of Proposition 7.1 and, towards a contradiction, suppose the sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(X^n)_{n \ge 1}$$\end{document}(Xn)n≥1 is not tight in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\textbf{D}_{{\mathbb {R}}^d}[0,\infty ), d_{J_1})$$\end{document}(DRd[0,∞),dJ1). Consider a lack of the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$J_1$$\end{document}J1 tightness condition based on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\hat{w}}$$\end{document}w^ as specified in [5, Thm. 12.4]. \cap \;\{ {\hat{w}}_{n^{-1}}(X^n,X^n)\,> \, \eta \} \; \subseteq \; \{\tau ^n_q \, > \, T\} \; \cap \; \bigcup _{k=1}^{q} \Lambda ^n_k \; . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}{τqn>T}∩{w^n-1(Xn,Xn)>η}⊆{τqn>T}∩⋃k=1qΛkn.Once this has been done, using (7.4) and then combining (7.7) and (7.6), we immediately obtain the desired contradiction as follows:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \varepsilon \; Case 1: If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|X^n_{\tau ^n_\ell }-X^n_s|>\eta /2$$\end{document}|Xτℓnn-Xsn|>η/2, then, by definition of ℓ ℓ, it must hold that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau ^n_{\ell +1}=s$$\end{document}τℓ+1n=s and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s<\tau ^n_{\ell +2} \le t$$\end{document}s<τℓ+2n≤t. Thus, we obtain \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s<\tau ^n_{\ell +3}\le u$$\end{document}s<τℓ+3n≤u, which yields \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ell \le q-4$$\end{document}ℓ≤q-4 with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\tau _{\ell +1}^n -\tau _{\ell +2}^n| \le \theta _2 $$\end{document}|τℓ+1n-τℓ+2n|≤θ2 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\tau _{\ell +3}^n -\tau _{\ell +2}^n| \le \theta _2 $$\end{document}|τℓ+3n-τℓ+2n|≤θ2, so one readily deduces from the definition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Lambda ^n_{\ell +2}$$\end{document}Λℓ+2n in (7.6) that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega \in \Lambda ^n_{\ell +2}$$\end{document}ω∈Λℓ+2n.

Case 2: If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|X^n_{\tau ^n_\ell }-X^n_s|\le \eta /2$$\end{document}|Xτℓnn-Xsn|≤η/2, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|X^n_{\tau ^n_\ell }- X^n_t| > \eta /2$$\end{document}|Xτℓnn-Xtn|>η/2 since otherwise, again by the triangle inequality, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|X^n_s- X^n_t|\le \eta $$\end{document}|Xsn-Xtn|≤η. In the first case, we proceed as above to obtain \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s<\tau ^n_{\ell +2}\le u$$\end{document}s<τℓ+2n≤u and therefore \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega \in \Lambda ^n_{\ell +1}$$\end{document}ω∈Λℓ+1n. For the second case, it follows immediately that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s<\tau ^n_{\ell +2}\le t$$\end{document}s<τℓ+2n≤t and, by same argument as before, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s<\tau ^n_{\ell +3}\le u$$\end{document}s<τℓ+3n≤u with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ell \le q-4$$\end{document}ℓ≤q-4, which yields \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega \in \Lambda ^n_{\ell +2}$$\end{document}ω∈Λℓ+2n. □ □

Equipped with Proposition 7.1, we can now proceed to the proof of Theorem 3.4.

To see this, we proceed in three steps:

Step 1: We begin by considering the set A, for which we claim that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A\cap \Gamma ^\complement =\emptyset $$\end{document}A∩Γ∁=∅. Consequently, we obtain \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert Z_{\tau } - [ Z_{(\tau -s)\vee 0}, Z_{\sigma }] \Vert> \eta _1 > \eta _2$$\end{document}‖Zτ-[Z(τ-s)∨0,Zσ]‖>η1>η2, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left\| \cdot \right\| $$\end{document}· is defined as in Definition A.7. This however yields that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{w''_\theta (Z)\le \eta _2\} \cap \{ {{\bar{w}}}^{\uparrow }_{n^{-1}}(Z) > \eta _1 \}\cap \Gamma ^\complement =\emptyset $$\end{document}{wθ′′(Z)≤η2}∩{w¯-1↑(Z)>η1}∩Γ∁=∅ and so we deduce \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A\cap \Gamma ^\complement =\emptyset $$\end{document}A∩Γ∁=∅. \, > \, \; {\mathbb {E}}[ Z_{{{\tilde{\tau }}}} {{\textbf{1}}}_A] \; + \; \eta _1 {\mathbb {P}}(A) \; \ge \; {\mathbb {E}}[ Z_{{{\tilde{\tau }}}} {{\textbf{1}}}_A] \; + \; \frac{3\varepsilon _1\eta _1}{4}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}E[Zσ~1A]>E[Zτ~1A]+η1P(A)≥E[Zτ~1A]+3ε1η14.Step 2: On \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B\cap \Gamma ^\complement $$\end{document}B∩Γ∁, the definition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\tilde{\tau }}}, {{\tilde{\sigma }}}$$\end{document}τ~,σ~ yields \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Z_{{{\tilde{\sigma }}}}-Z_{{{\tilde{\tau }}}}=Z_T-Z_T=0$$\end{document}Zσ~-Zτ~=ZT-ZT=0. Hence,7.14\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {\mathbb {E}}\left[ \, Z_{{{\tilde{\sigma }}}} \, {{\textbf{1}}}_B \, \right] \; \ge \; {\mathbb {E}}\left[ \, Z_{{{\tilde{\tau }}}} \, {{\textbf{1}}}_B\, \right] \; - \; \frac{\varepsilon _1\eta _1}{4}.\end{aligned}$$\end{document}EZσ~1B≥EZτ~1B-ε1η14.Step 3: Lastly, recalling \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C=\{w''_{\theta }(Z) > \eta _2 \}$$\end{document}C={wθ′′(Z)>η2}, since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathbb {P}}(C)< \varepsilon _2 \le \delta $$\end{document}P(C)<ε2≤δ due to (7.10), we obtain, on behalf of the uniform integrability bound (7.11), the inequality\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {\mathbb {E}}\left[ (Z_{{{\tilde{\tau }}}}-Z_{{{\tilde{\sigma }}}} ) {{\textbf{1}}}_C \right] \, \le \, {\mathbb {E}}\left[ |Z_{{{\tilde{\sigma }}}}| {{\textbf{1}}}_C \right] \, + \, {\mathbb {E}}\left[ |Z_{{{\tilde{\tau }}}}| {{\textbf{1}}}_C \right] \, \le \, \frac{\varepsilon _1\eta _1}{8} \, + \, \frac{\varepsilon _1\eta _1}{8} \, = \, \frac{\varepsilon _1 \eta _1}{4} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}E(Zτ~-Zσ~)1C≤E|Zσ~|1C+E|Zτ~|1C≤ε1η18+ε1η18=ε1η14and, in turn, we get7.15\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {\mathbb {E}}\left[ \, Z_{{{\tilde{\sigma }}}} \, {{\textbf{1}}}_C \, \right] \; Hence, the family \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{X^i : i \in I\}$$\end{document}{Xi:i∈I} cannot be relatively compact for the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M_1$$\end{document}M1 topology. □ □

Proof of Proposition 3.6

Proof of Proposition 3.6

Denote \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb{F}^n:=({\mathcal {F}}^n_t)_{t\ge 0}$$\end{document}Fn:=(Ftn)t≥0 the natural filtrations generated by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(H^n,M^n)$$\end{document}(Hn,Mn). Without loss of generality, we may assume the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M^n$$\end{document}Mn to be martingales. Indeed, by the local martingale property, there exist \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb{F}^n$$\end{document}Fn-stopping times \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sigma ^n$$\end{document}σn such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M^n_{\bullet \wedge \sigma ^n}$$\end{document}M∙∧σnn are martingales and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathbb {P}}^n(\sigma ^n\le T) \rightarrow 0$$\end{document}Pn(σn≤T)→0 as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n\rightarrow \infty $$\end{document}n→∞ for all T>0T>0. Hence, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M^n_{\bullet \wedge \sigma ^n}\Rightarrow X$$\end{document}M∙∧σnn⇒X on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\textbf{D}_{{\mathbb {R}}^d}[0,\infty ),d_{M_1})$$\end{document}(DRd[0,∞),dM1) since convergence in the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M_1$$\end{document}M1 topology on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{D}_{{\mathbb {R}}^d}[0,\infty )$$\end{document}DRd[0,∞) is uniquely characterised by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M_1$$\end{document}M1 convergence on almost all compacts. Next, condition (3.2) ensures the localised uniform integrability of the local martingales \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M^n$$\end{document}Mn on compact time intervals (as in Definition 3.3), and therefore, by Corollary 3.5, there is convergence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(H^n,M^n)\Rightarrow (H,X)$$\end{document}(Hn,Mn)⇒(H,X) on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\textbf{D}_{{\mathbb {R}}^d}[0,\infty ),d_{M_1})\times (\textbf{D}_{{\mathbb {R}}^d}[0,\infty ),d_{J_1})$$\end{document}(DRd[0,∞),dM1)×(DRd[0,∞),dJ1). For any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha \in \textbf{D}_{{\mathbb {R}}^d}[0,\infty )$$\end{document}α∈DRd[0,∞) and c>0c>0 define \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rho _c(\alpha ):= \text {inf}\{t>0: |\alpha (t)|\ge c \text { or } |\alpha (t-)|\ge c\}$$\end{document}ρc(α):=inf{t>0:|α(t)|≥cor|α(t-)|≥c}. As described at the beginning of the proof of [20, Prop. IX.1.17], there exists a co-countable subset \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C\subseteq [0,\infty )$$\end{document}C⊆[0,∞) such that for all c∈ Cc∈C it holds that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(H^n,M^n_{\bullet \wedge \rho _c(M^n)})\Rightarrow (H,X_{\bullet \wedge \rho _c(X)})$$\end{document}(Hn,M∙∧ρc(Mn)n)⇒(H,X∙∧ρc(X)) on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\textbf{D}_{{\mathbb {R}}^d}[0,\infty ),d_{M_1})\times (\textbf{D}_{{\mathbb {R}}^d}[0,\infty ),d_{J_1})$$\end{document}(DRd[0,∞),dM1)×(DRd[0,∞),dJ1). Finally, let c∈ Cc∈C. Following the argument in the proof of [20, Prop. IX.1.12] (with the definition of quantities as in the proof of [20, Prop. IX.1.10]) we first deduce the uniform integrability on compacts of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$X_{\bullet \wedge \rho _c(X)}$$\end{document}X∙∧ρc(X) from (3.2), and then its martingale property in terms of the filtration generated by (H, X). □ □

Proofs of the results from section 5

Proof of Proposition 5.2

Proof of Proposition 5.2

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau ^n_c$$\end{document}τcn be stopping times defined as in the definition of (GD), and let K>0K>0. Furthermore, let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rho ^n_c:= \text {inf}\{t>0: n^{-\beta }N_{nt}>c\}$$\end{document}ρcn:=inf{t>0:n-βNnt>c}. The martingality of the processes \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$X^n$$\end{document}Xn is shown in [46, Prop. 3.3]. Clearly, the stopping times \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sigma ^n_c:= \tau ^n_c\wedge \rho ^n_c$$\end{document}σcn:=τcn∧ρcn are a localising sequence as in Definition 3.3. For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0\le t \le T$$\end{document}0≤t≤T and K>2cK>2c it holds that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {\mathbb {E}}\Big [|X^n_{t\wedge \sigma ^n_c}| {{\textbf{1}}}_{\{|X^n_{t\wedge \sigma ^n_c}|>K\}}\Big ] \; \le \; c \, {\mathbb {P}}\big (|X^n|^*_{T}>K\big ) \,+ \, {\mathbb {E}}\Big [|\Delta X^n_{t\wedge \sigma ^n_c}| {{\textbf{1}}}_{\{|\Delta X^n_{t\wedge \sigma ^n_c}|>K/2\}}\Big ]. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}E[|Xt∧σcnn|1{|Xt∧σcnn|>K}]≤cP(|Xn|T∗>K)+E[|ΔXt∧σcnn|1{|ΔXt∧σcnn|>K/2}].The first summand on the right-hand side converges to zero, uniformly in n, as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K\rightarrow \infty $$\end{document}K→∞ due to the tightness of the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$X^n$$\end{document}Xn on the Skorokhod space. For the second summand, we note that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}{\mathbb {E}}\Big [|\Delta X^n_{t\wedge \sigma ^n_c}| {{\textbf{1}}}_{\{|\Delta X^n_{t\wedge \sigma ^n_c}|>K/2\}}\Big ] \,&\le \, \frac{1}{n^{-\frac{\beta }{\alpha }}} \sum _{j=1}^{cn^\beta } {\mathbb {E}}\Big [ |\theta _j| {{\textbf{1}}}_{\{ |\theta _j|> n^{\frac{\beta }{\alpha }} K/2\}}\Big ] \\ &= \, \frac{cn^\beta }{n^{-\frac{\beta }{\alpha }}} {\mathbb {E}}\Big [ |\theta _0|^{\alpha -\gamma } {{\textbf{1}}}_{\{ |\theta _0|> n^{\frac{\beta }{\alpha }} K/2\}}\Big ] \end{aligned}$$\end{document}E[|ΔXt∧σcnn|1{|ΔXt∧σcnn|>K/2}]≤1n-βα∑j=1cnβE[|θj|1{|θj|>nβαK/2}]=cnβn-βαE[|θ0|α-γ1{|θ0|>nβαK/2}]with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\gamma :=\alpha -1>0$$\end{document}γ:=α-1>0. Construction of the counterexample in Proposition 5.3

Proof of Proposition 5.3

We split the construction into four steps.

Step 1: We can rewrite the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$X^n$$\end{document}Xn as\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} X^n_t \; = \; \frac{1}{n^{\frac{1}{\alpha }}} \; \frac{1}{c_0+c_1} \; \sum _{k=1}^{\lfloor nt \rfloor } \; (c_0\theta _k + c_1 \theta _{k-1}) \; = \; \frac{1}{n^{\frac{1}{\alpha }}} \; \sum _{k=1}^{\lfloor nt \rfloor } \; \theta _k \; - \; \frac{1}{n^{\frac{1}{\alpha }}}\, \frac{c_1}{c_0+c_1} \, ( \theta _{\lfloor n t\rfloor } - \theta _0) \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Xtn=1n1α1c0+c1∑k=1⌊nt⌋(c0θk+c1θk-1)=1n1α∑k=1⌊nt⌋θk-1n1αc1c0+c1(θ⌊nt⌋-θ0)and we define \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A^n_t:=n^{-\frac{1}{\alpha }} \sum _{k=1}^{\lfloor nt \rfloor } \theta _k$$\end{document}Atn:=n-1α∑k=1⌊nt⌋θk and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B^n_t:=c_1 n^{-\frac{1}{\alpha }} (c_0+c_1)^{-1} ( \theta _{\lfloor n t\rfloor } - \theta _0)$$\end{document}Btn:=c1n-1α(c0+c1)-1(θ⌊nt⌋-θ0). It is well-known that the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A^n$$\end{document}An converge weakly in the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$J_1$$\end{document}J1 topology to an α α-stable Lévy process A if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathbb {E}}[\theta _0]=0$$\end{document}E[θ0]=0 (see (5.5)). In particular, its f.d.d. converge to zero. Towards a contradiction, suppose the f.d.d. of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\int _0^\bullet H^n_{s-} \textrm{d}X^n_s)_{n\ge 1}$$\end{document}(∫0∙Hs-ndXsn)n≥1 are tight, and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$D\subseteq [0,\infty )$$\end{document}D⊆[0,∞) be countable. Step 3: the integrands. Step 4: deriving a contradiction. From the tightness of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$({\hat{B}}_n)_{n\ge 1}$$\end{document}(B^n)n≥1, to every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta < \frac{1}{2}$$\end{document}δ<12, we obtain the existence of a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K_\delta >0$$\end{document}Kδ>0 such that8.3\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {\mathbb {P}}\Big ( \, \frac{c}{n^{\frac{1}{\alpha }+\varepsilon }}\; \sum _{k=2}^{\lfloor n s\rfloor -1} \, |\theta _k| \, {{\textbf{1}}}_{\{\text {sgn}(\theta _k)\, \ne \, \text {sgn}(\theta _{k-1})\}} \; > \; K_\delta \Big ) \; \le \; \delta \end{aligned}$$\end{document}P(cn1α+ε∑k=2⌊ns⌋-1|θk|1{sgn(θk)≠sgn(θk-1)}>Kδ)≤δfor all n\ge 1n≥1, due to (8.2).
